class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10 ** 9 + 7
        N = len(s)
        c1 = s.count('1')
        if not c1:
            return (((N-1)*(N-2))//2) % MOD
        if c1 % 3:
            return 0
        idx1, idx2, idx3, idx4 = 0, 0, 0, 0
        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
            if cnt == c1//3:
                idx1 = i
                break
        for i in range(idx1+1,N):
            if s[i] == '0':
                idx2 = i
                break
        for i in range(N-1,-1,-1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
            if cnt == c1//3:
                idx4 = i
                break
        for i in range(idx4-1,-1,-1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx3 = i
                break
        return ((idx2-idx1) * (idx4-idx3)) % MOD

```python
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10 ** 9 + 7
        N = len(s)
        c1 = s.count('1')
        if not c1:
            return (((N-1)*(N-2))//2) % MOD
        if c1 % 3:
            return 0
        idx1, idx2, idx3, idx4 = 0, 0, 0, 0
        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
            if cnt == c1//3:
                idx1 = i
                break
        for i in range(idx1+1,N):
            if s[i] == '0':
                idx2 = i
                break

                cnt += 1

                idx4 = i
                break
        for i in range(idx4-1,-1,-1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx3 = i
                break
        return ((idx2-idx1) * (idx4-idx3)) % MOD
```

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

```
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9+7
        ones = [i for i,c in enumerate(s) if c=='1']
        m = len(ones)
        n = len(s)
        if m == 0:
            return ((n-1)*(n-2)//2) % MOD
        if m % 3:
            return 0
        k = m // 3
        return ((ones[k] - ones[k-1]) * (ones[2*k] - ones[2*k-1])) % MOD
```

```
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        n = len(s)
        ones = [i for i, ch in enumerate(s) if ch == '1']
        total = len(ones)

        if total == 0:
            return ((n - 1) * (n - 2) // 2) % MOD
        if total % 3 != 0:
            return 0

        k = total // 3
        return ((ones[k] - ones[k - 1]) * (ones[2 * k] - ones[2 * k - 1])) % MOD
```
Need ensure indices valid: total>=3, k>=1. ones[k] exists because k <= total-1? total=3k, k <= 3k-1 for k>=1 yes. ones[2k] exists? 2k <= 3k-1 for k>=1 yes. Good.

```
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        N = len(s)
        c1 = s.count('1')
        if not c1:
            return (((N-1)*(N-2))//2) % MOD
        if c1 % 3:
            return 0
        k = c1 // 3
        idx1 = idx2 = idx3 = idx4 = -1
        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx1 = i
                elif cnt == k + 1:
                    idx2 = i
                    break
        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx4 = i
                elif cnt == k + 1:
                    idx3 = i
                    break
        return ((idx2 - idx1) * (idx4 - idx3)) % MOD
```

Let's ensure final code is syntactically correct:
```
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10 ** 9 + 7
        n = len(s)
        ones = [i for i, ch in enumerate(s) if ch == '1']
        total = len(ones)

        if total == 0:
            return ((n - 1) * (n - 2) // 2) % MOD

        if total % 3 != 0:
            return 0

        k = total // 3
        return ((ones[k] - ones[k - 1]) * (ones[2 * k] - ones[2 * k - 1])) % MOD
```
Potential issue: If total=0, n maybe 0? Not allowed. If n=1, (0 * -1)//2 = 0? In Python, 0 * -1 = 0, //2 =0. If n=2, (1*0)//2=0. Good. If n=0, (-1 * -2)//2 = 1? But not possible. Fine.

Could also avoid building list to save memory? But list length up to 1e5, fine. The original code uses O(1) memory. But LeetCode memory okay. class Solution:
```
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10 ** 9 + 7
        N = len(s)
        c1 = s.count('1')
        if not c1:
            return (((N-1)*(N-2))//2) % MOD
        if c1 % 3:
            return 0
        idx1, idx2, idx3, idx4 = 0, 0, 0, 0
        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
            if cnt == c1//3:
                idx1 = i
                break
        for i in range(idx1+1,N):
            if s[i] == '0':
                idx2 = i
                break
        for i in range(N-1,-1,-1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
            if cnt == c1//3:
                idx4 = i
                break
        for i in range(idx4-1,-1,-1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx3 = i
                break
```

```
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10 ** 9 + 7
        N = len(s)
        c1 = s.count('1')
        if not c1:
            return (((N - 1) * (N - 2)) // 2) % MOD
        if c1 % 3:
            return 0

        k = c1 // 3
        idx1 = idx2 = idx3 = idx4 = -1

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx1 = i
                elif cnt == k + 1:
                    idx2 = i
                    break

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx4 = i
                elif cnt == k + 1:
                    idx3 = i
                    break

        return ((idx2 - idx1) * (idx4 - idx3)) % MOD
```

Let's write fixed original carefully:

```
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10 ** 9 + 7
        N = len(s)
        c1 = s.count('1')
        if not c1:
            return (((N - 1) * (N - 2)) // 2) % MOD
        if c1 % 3:
            return 0

        k = c1 // 3

        idx1 = idx2 = idx3 = idx4 = -1

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx1 = i
                elif cnt == k + 1:
                    idx2 = i
                    break

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx4 = i
                elif cnt == k + 1:
                    idx3 = i
                    break

        return ((idx2 - idx1) * (idx4 - idx3)) % MOD
```

Let's write final code with no comments:
```
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10 ** 9 + 7
        N = len(s)
        c1 = s.count('1')

        if not c1:
            return (((N - 1) * (N - 2)) // 2) % MOD
        if c1 % 3:
            return 0

        k = c1 // 3

        idx1 = idx2 = idx3 = idx4 = -1

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx1 = i
                    break

        for i in range(idx1 + 1, N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx2 = i
                break

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx4 = i
                    break

        for i in range(idx4 - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx3 = i
                break

        return ((idx2 - idx1) * (idx4 - idx3)) % MOD
```

Let's ensure final code exactly:
```
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10 ** 9 + 7
        N = len(s)
        c1 = s.count('1')

        if not c1:
            return (((N - 1) * (N - 2)) // 2) % MOD
        if c1 % 3:
            return 0

        k = c1 // 3

        idx1 = idx2 = idx3 = idx4 = -1

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx1 = i
                    break

        for i in range(idx1 + 1, N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx2 = i
                break

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx4 = i
                    break

        for i in range(idx4 - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx3 = i
                break

        return ((idx2 - idx1) * (idx4 - idx3)) % MOD
```
Let's think if `idx1` and `idx4` are set before using in loops. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `idx2` loop doesn't find a one because of a bug, then idx2 remains -1 and return negative. But not possible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `idx4 - idx3` maybe zero? If p_{2k} == p_{2k-1}? Impossible because distinct positions. At least 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if `idx2 - idx1` maybe zero? Impossible. Good.

```
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10 ** 9 + 7
        N = len(s)
        c1 = s.count('1')

        if not c1:
            return (((N - 1) * (N - 2)) // 2) % MOD
        if c1 % 3:
            return 0

        k = c1 // 3

        idx1 = idx2 = idx3 = idx4 = -1

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx1 = i
                    break

        for i in range(idx1 + 1, N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx2 = i
                break

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx4 = i
                    break

        for i in range(idx4 - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx3 = i
                break

        return ((idx2 - idx1) * (idx4 - idx3)) % MOD
```

But wait, in the first loop, we set idx1 = kth one and break. Then idx2 loop finds next one = (k+1)th one. Return idx2-idx1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if the string has ones count divisible by 3 but c1=

=== FIXED CODE ===
class Solution:
    def numWays(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10 ** 9 + 7
        N = len(s)
        c1 = s.count('1')

        if not c1:
            return (((N - 1) * (N - 2)) // 2) % MOD
        if c1 % 3:
            return 0

        k = c1 // 3

        idx1 = idx2 = idx3 = idx4 = -1

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx1 = i
                    break

        for i in range(idx1 + 1, N):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx2 = i
                break

        cnt = 0
        for i in range(N - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                cnt += 1
                if cnt == k:
                    idx4 = i
                    break

        for i in range(idx4 - 1, -1, -1):
            if s[i] == '1':
                idx3 = i
                break

        return ((idx2 - idx1) * (idx4 - idx3)) % MOD
